**Notes for app integration**
- This transcript addendum is meant to be incorporated as a raw transcript to supplement the structured rule-set and should be used alongside the earlier formatted knowledge-base entries for retrieval and answer synthesis.
- The teacher’s language is highly oral and at times repetitive; when used in production retrieval, it should be treated as a source transcript, not a strict editorial summary.
- For retrieval quality, the structured summary above is the primary source; the raw transcript should be kept as backup/context, not as the only canonical form.


**12.7 Venus in the 7th house — gender-differentiated reading**
- **In a male chart**: considered a poor placement — associated with high libido, flirtatiousness, and multiple relationships.
- **In a female chart**: considered a very favorable placement — associated with a Rajayoga-like outcome, a good, loving, and physically attractive husband, and a generally good married life. Noted downside: the husband's attractiveness may attract unwanted attention from other women, creating a secondary social friction, but this is framed as a minor complication rather than a serious flaw.
- Classical reading: native is compassionate, skilled/clever in intimate matters, valorous, well-versed in the arts, has attractive features, and is well-dressed/adorned (associated with social respect/honor).

**12.8 Saturn in the 7th house**
- Classically summarized via a Sanskrit phrase (paraphrased): tends to give one or more of — an **older spouse** (Vayodhika), a **spouse prone to illness/disease** (Rogini), or a **spouse uninterested in the marital/worldly aspects of the relationship** and inclined toward asceticism/spiritual pursuits (Tapasvini) — the ascetic-inclined variant can manifest either as the spouse pursuing spiritual development seriously (sometimes at the expense of marital/household duties) or otherwise withdrawing from worldly marital engagement.
- **Exception**: if Saturn is placed in its own sign in the 7th house (Capricorn or Aquarius) → these negative outcomes do not manifest; instead (a rare but notably positive outcome) the couple can have a long, deeply loving marriage lasting many decades (up to a "platinum jubilee"-length marriage).

**12.9 Rahu in the 7th house**
- Not considered favorable. Associated with "falling from one's clan" — can manifest as an inter-caste or international/cross-cultural marriage, and/or bringing some form of shame or reputational difficulty to the family via the nature of the marital/relationship situation (not necessarily an affair by the native themselves — can also mean marrying someone whose background/conduct becomes a source of family embarrassment).
- **Exception**: if the 7th lord is itself placed in the 7th house and is conjoined with Rahu there, the negative reading does not apply — instead, Rahu amplifies the (already positive) result of the 7th lord's own placement in its own house, per the general principle that Rahu/Ketu amplify whatever they're connected to (see Rule 4.4/10.4 context on Rahu-Ketu amplification).

**12.10 Ketu in the 7th house**
- Associated with either a general disinterest in marriage, or (alternatively) more than one marriage. Ketu is generally considered favorable for marriage matters overall — but specifically less favorable when placed directly in the 7th house itself.

**12.11 Combined multi-planet influence — exceptional beauty/desirability**
- Moon (in a favorable sign like Cancer), combined with Mercury, Jupiter, and Mars all influencing the 7th house together → indicates exceptional physical attractiveness, described as capable of making even ascetics fall in love.
- Separately, Mars connected to (aspecting or placed in) the **Ascendant** (not the 7th house specifically) → makes the native a general center of attraction for others (a magnetic/attractive personal presence), applicable in both male and female charts.

---

## 13. Planet-by-Planet Effects: Planet Placed in the 8th House (Marriage-Relevant)

- **Venus or Jupiter in the 8th house** → indicates loss or denial of progeny (children); does not by itself indicate a marriage problem.
- **Mars in the 8th house** → tends toward flirtatious tendencies.
- **Moon in the 8th house** → tends to divide/reduce marital happiness.
- **Saturn in the 8th house** → tends to give a spouse who is diseased/unwell.
- **Sun in the 8th house** → tends to make the native themselves prone to illness/disease.
- **Rahu in the 8th house** → associated with a lack of moral restraint (characterless conduct) and a tendency to break family norms/traditions, bringing a bad name to the family.
  - **Exception**: if this Rahu is Vargottama, the result becomes positive rather than negative.

---

## 14. 7th Lord's Navamsha Placement — Spouse Characteristics

Where the native's 7th house lord is placed in the Navamsha (D9) chart shapes the qualitative nature of the spouse:

| 7th lord's Navamsha sign/planet | Spouse characteristics indicated |
|---|---|
| Saturn's Navamsha (Capricorn/Aquarius) | Older spouse; marriage brings a form of misery/hardship (reading follows Saturn's general significations, not the literal signs Capricorn/Aquarius — see methodological note below) |
| Mars's Navamsha (Aries/Scorpio) | Angry but devoted spouse. If Mars is *weak* (e.g., debilitated in D1) → spouse instead tends toward having affairs, rather than simply being angry-but-devoted |
| Venus's Navamsha (Taurus/Libra) | Spouse has good looks and good bodily form; spouse is fond of/devoted to the native; native is fortunate in marriage; spouse is also fortunate in their own life/profession |
| Mercury's Navamsha (Gemini/Virgo) | Spouse is learned, clever, skillful, and knowledgeable |
| Moon's Navamsha (Cancer) | Spouse is passionate but reserved/quiet about expressing feelings |
| Jupiter's Navamsha (Sagittarius/Pisces) | Spouse is brave, a risk-taker, unafraid to speak up (including in defense of the native), exercises self-control over passions/desires despite having them, and possesses many good qualities — a comparatively rare and highly positive combination |
| Sun's Navamsha (Leo) | Spouse is notably mild-tempered (contrast: Sun *placed in* the 7th house directly, per Rule 12.2, gives an angry spouse — the Navamsha placement of the 7th lord gives the opposite, mild temperament); spouse is multi-skilled, engages in multiple professions, and is highly inclined toward intimate/sexual matters (high libido) |
| Mercury's Navamsha (younger-spouse note) | Also associated with a younger spouse (alongside Mars's Navamsha, below) |
| Mars's Navamsha (younger-spouse note) | Associated with a younger spouse (compared to the native) |

**14.1 Methodological note — planets vs. signs (important distinction stressed at length by the teacher)**
- The teacher strongly emphasizes that Navamsha/divisional-chart results belong to the **ruling planet** of that division, not to the sign that planet happens to rule. For example, the reading for "7th lord in Saturn's Navamsha" comes from Saturn's own significations (misery, hardship, restriction) — **not** from Capricorn's or Aquarius's significations as signs. Houses, signs, and planets are each stated to carry distinct, non-interchangeable significations, and should never be conflated when interpreting divisional charts.

**14.2 Contradictory-indicator resolution rule**
- When two indicators conflict (e.g., 7th lord's Navamsha suggests an older spouse via Saturn, but a planet placed directly in the 7th house suggests a younger spouse via Mars) — determine which of the two factors is stronger (by standard strength criteria: own sign, exaltation, etc.) and let the stronger factor's result prevail; the weaker factor's indication is effectively overridden/nullified.

---

## 15. Single vs. Multiple Marriage — Additional Combinations

**15.1 "One life, one wife" combinations (single-marriage indicators)**
- Jupiter and Mercury both placed in the 7th house → indicates only one marriage.
- 7th lord placed in the Navamsha of Leo, Aries, or Scorpio (i.e., in the Navamsha ruled by Sun or Mars) → indicates only one marriage.
- **Important caveat**: these are exceptions that override *multiple-marriage* combinations specifically — they restrict the number of *marriages* to one, but do not necessarily prevent affairs; if the underlying multiplicity combination is otherwise strong, it may still manifest as an affair even while the marriage itself remains singular.
- If the 7th lord is exalted while in Aries/Scorpio/Leo Navamsha specifically → the single-marriage reading is reinforced (only one marriage, even with an otherwise strong 7th lord).

**15.2 Physically challenged spouse combination**
- Sun and Moon conjoined in the 6th house, OR Sun and Moon conjoined in the 12th house, OR Sun in 6th with Moon in 12th (or vice versa) in mutual aspect; OR Mars and Venus conjoined in the 5th, 7th, or 9th house → associated with a physically challenged spouse.

---

## 16. Divorce Analysis

**16.1 Primary divorce indicator — 8th house**
- A natural malefic placed in the 8th house is described as a strong, largely unavertable indicator of divorce risk. No planet in the 8th house, or a benefic placed there, generally protects against this.
- If the 8th lord goes to a Navamsha ruled by a malefic planet → creates roughly 50-50 odds of divorce (a moderating rather than a determining factor); otherwise, divorce risk from this specific pathway is not strongly indicated.

**16.2 Protective/cancelling factors for divorce**
- A benefic influencing the 2nd house → can avert divorce even in the presence of an otherwise afflicted 8th house (note: in a more extreme case, an afflicted 8th house with no 2nd-house protection can indicate death of the spouse rather than divorce — occurring very late in life, e.g., around 80-85 years of age, if other factors are mild).
- A strong/powerful 2nd house lord → can also avert divorce.
- Ketu is described as generally the single most protective/benefic planet for marriage matters overall ("Ketu patak shaadi bachata" — Ketu saves marriages) — described as acting without ego/conflict-generating tendencies.

**16.3 Matchmaking as divorce-risk mitigation**
- Practical matchmaking guidance given: if the native's own chart shows a divorce-risk combination, the ideal spouse match is someone whose chart does *not* also show a divorce-risk combination — pairing two divorce-prone charts together compounds the risk, similar to the teacher's analogy of two blind people being poorly matched to each other.

**16.4 Planetary war**
- If a planet relevant to the 7th house analysis is involved in a planetary war (graha yuddha) with another planet, this is described as highly detrimental — positive results from that planet's placement should not be predicted; only negative/reduced results should be anticipated, with negative outcomes read as intensified.

**16.5 Papakartari Yoga on the 7th house/lord**
- 7th house hemmed between malefics (Papakartari on the house itself) → indicates death of the spouse, potentially by an accident involving a sharp object, road accident, or a fall from height.
- **Distinction**: Papakartari affecting the 7th house itself (as a house) indicates the spouse's death occurring through circumstances not directly tied to the native's own actions. Papakartari affecting Venus specifically (as karaka/significator for spouse) indicates the spouse's death occurring in a manner where the native feels a sense of guilt or perceived responsibility (e.g., an incident that happens while the spouse is with/because of the native).
- Note: Papakartari affliction principles apply to significators (karakas) and to houses; they are stated as not applicable to house lords in the same way.

**16.6 Multiple marriages — reading each from the 7th house**
- All marriages (first, second, etc.) should be analyzed from the 7th house consistently — not, for example, reading a second marriage from the 9th house (which the teacher notes would imply a confusing/rare interpretation, such as one's father and husband being signified by the same house).

---

## 17. Remedies (Part 2 Additions)

**17.1 Reframing donation as gain, not loss**
- The teacher explicitly stresses that donation-based remedies should not be understood as "losing" something — they are framed as a means of gaining positive outcomes, not sacrifice.

**17.2 Kama-Rati worship (for marital/romantic vitality)**
- Kama (god of desire/romantic love) and Rati (his consort) are described using the concept of "kama" as *mutual, reciprocal exchange* between partners (giving care and receiving equivalent care back), distinct from "artha" (a transactional, unequal exchange, e.g., paying for a service).
- **Vasant Panchami** (not Saraswati Puja, despite common association) is identified as the specific day associated with Kama/Rati worship for this purpose.
- Specific remedy: bathe a Shivling with **sugarcane juice** — described as very effective for marriage-related goals. Alternative: offering sugarcane at temples of Lalita, Bala, or Bhuvaneshwari (forms of the Divine Mother associated specifically with supporting/blessing Kama, as distinct from other forms like Kali, Tara, or Chinnamasta, which are described as controlling/subduing desire rather than supporting it).
- Additional remedy: **donation of green leafy vegetables**, ideally performed on Panchami (the day associated with Kama), in the evening.

**17.3 Silver/copper/gold coin donation with a wish (barter-style remedy)**
- Take a silver, copper, or gold coin; hold it while making a specific wish (e.g., for a child's marriage to happen), with a paired commitment (e.g., "if this wish is fulfilled, I will donate/feed X poor people" or a similar act of gratitude); place the coin in a temple donation box. Once the wish is fulfilled, the promised follow-up act of gratitude must be completed. Framed explicitly as a reciprocal "barter" with the divine.

---

## 18. Lagna, Graha Worship, Time-Throne, and Mantra Foundations (Transcript Addendum)

**18.1 Gemstone use for the Lagna Lord: intelligence vs. power**
- The teacher explicitly warns against treating Lagna-lord gemstones as a generic “make the chart strong” tool aimed at increasing raw power or fighting energy.
- For the Lagna, the point is not simply “strength” in a combative sense; the Lagna is primarily linked to intelligence, buddhi, clarity, and the quality of the mind.
- Wearing a gemstone for the Lagna lord to boost intelligence is considered a limited and external method; the teacher strongly prefers inner purification and mantra practice, especially Gayatri, as a more effective route to intelligence and maturity.
- Practical guidance given: if a planet sits in the Lagna, first worship that planet; if no planet is in the Lagna, then worship the divine principle represented by the Lagna itself (the teacher associates this with Jagannath / Hari as the time-throne lord).

**18.2 The Lagna as divine throne / Jagannath principle**
- The Lagna is not just a house of personality; it is conceptually linked to the “throne” or the field of self-expression (adhisthana) through which the living being operates in time.
- If no planet is in the Lagna, the teacher says “Jagannath is sitting on your head” — the divine principle is present as the primary spiritual authority of the Lagna itself.
- If a planet is in the Lagna, the native should first honor that planet, then honor the Lagna as a higher field of divine authority.
- The teacher uses the concept of the “throne of the world” as temporary and passing: kings, ministers, and rulers all eventually leave the throne, but the divine principle remains the true seat of authority in time.

**18.3 Krishna and Rama as one time-throne principle**
- The teacher frames the “throne of the world” as occupied by an avatar in a given yuga, and in Kali Yuga specifically by Krishna.
- He presents Rama and Krishna as one divine principle manifesting in different forms, not as two unrelated personalities.
- The same principle is described as the same Hari in different manifestations; the difference is in the degree of manifestation and energetic intensity of the avatara.
- The teacher links this to the 10th house as the natural “throne” or time-house, especially Capricorn (Makara), which becomes the seat of the avataric time principle in the zodiac.

**18.4 Capricorn / 10th house as the time-throne**
- The 10th house is described as the most important Kendra and as the house of time, authority, and dharma-realization in the cycle of human life.
- Capricorn is therefore treated as the seat of Hari or the avataric principle in the zodiac.
- The teacher says this is why Makar Sankranti is spiritually significant: it marks the Sun’s entry into Capricorn, and the awakening of the avataric principle associated with the time-throne.
- He also connects this to the ritual performance in Puri, where 21 brahmins recite the Gayatri to awaken the avataric principle at the solar change in Capricorn.

**18.5 Forms of the avatar and the importance of the 10th house**
- The teacher presents the avatar as taking various forms/veshas (such as Hanuman, Vamana, etc.), but the underlying principle remains one.
- The 10th house is stated to be central because it governs time, duty, authority, and the actual conditions of life’s unfolding.
- The 10th house is therefore not merely “career” in a modern narrow sense; it is the field through which the soul experiences karma and the divine time order.
- Rahu in the 10th house is therefore not automatically “bad” in an absolute sense; it can become a pilgrim-making, guru-bringing, temple-going influence depending on the deeper context of the chart and the dasha.

**18.6 Happiness as the true criterion of remedial action**
- The teacher says that gemstone remedies should be chosen by a practical question: “Will this make me happy?”
- If the remedy increases misery, it is not the right remedy even if it is astrologically sophisticated.
- He places the criterion of happiness in the axis of the 4th and 10th houses: the comfort of the body and the stability of time/authority should support a person’s genuine happiness rather than merely symbolic external power.
- This is a critical corrective: a planet is not judged only by “strength” but by whether it supports the native’s life, peace, and capacity to function without being driven by fear or ego.

**18.7 The Sun–Moon polarity: direct and reverse counting**
- The teacher introduces a deep symbolic principle: the Sun rules the direct chain (Aries onward) and the Moon rules the reverse chain (Pisces backward), showing that the day/night division is not merely literal but structurally tied to the first principles of zodiacal order.
- Sun’s domain is the six signs from Aries through Virgo (day/solar order), while the Moon’s domain is Pisces through Libra (night/lunar order).
- This explains the preference for direct counting from Aries in the Sun’s domain and reverse counting from Pisces in the Moon’s domain.
- He explicitly ties this to the idea of Gandanta at the Pisces–Aries boundary, where the Moon’s movement toward the Sun’s domain encounters difficulty and is protected by the divine through Shiva’s intervention.

**18.8 Gandanta, Shiva, and the Moon’s protection**
- The teacher frames Gandanta as a threshold where the Moon’s motion and the Sun’s domain clash.
- The Moon is said to be saved or protected by a divine form (Shiva) who lifts the Moon and preserves it, creating the moon’s enduring role in sustaining life.
- This is used as a symbolic foundation for the Moon’s importance in longevity and the body’s continuity.
- In this logic, Shiva is not merely a mythic figure but the protector who stabilizes the Moon’s movement and thus the life-process itself.

**18.9 The Moon as the giver of longevity and life continuity**
- The teacher states that while the Sun is the giver of birth / Lagna/entry into life, the Moon is the indicator of life continuity, death timing, and the exit from the physical body.
- This is tied to the 12th house and the concept of the end of the chapter of life.
- He also says that after death, a new chart is formed; in this way, life is seen as a movement from one chart to another across the cycle of existence.
- Thus the Moon is linked to the subtle continuity of life, while the Sun governs the visible structural beginning.

**18.10 Lagna and the Sun: the root of birth**
- Lagna is calculated from the Sun’s movement and the sunrise framework; the Moon does not determine the Lagna in this specific logic.
- The teacher presents this as a foundational rule of chart construction: the moment of entry into life is tied to solar structure, while the Moon governs inner temperament and the emotional/mental layer.
- In the later spiritual framing, the Sun becomes the father principle, the divine power of the self, whereas the Moon is the mind’s fluctuating reflective field.

**18.11 Mantra length: Surya, Moon, and Gayatri**
- The teacher gives a practical mantra principle: the Sun corresponds to a seed/mantra structure connected to the Lagna and the solar cycle, with a 1-syllable or 24-syllable expression.
- Gayatri is the higher, complete manifestation of the solar principle, with the specific 24-syllable structure used to align with the pathway of the Lagna and the time-throne.
- The Moon is associated with the 12-syllable form (Dwadasakshari), because the Moon governs the mind and the fluctuating emotional field.
- The teacher also states that a planet in the Lagna can suggest a specific Gayatri or mantra of the corresponding deity, but the core principle is to keep the mantra aligned to the actual inner nature of the chart rather than using a generic mantra mechanically.

**18.12 Mind-surrender and the safe object of devotion**
- The teacher emphasizes that the mind should not be surrendered indiscriminately to living people, social ideals, films, or public personalities.
- The mind should instead be surrendered to a guru, a chosen avatara, or a stable divine principle.
- He specifically prefers the worship of Krishna / Vishnu-oriented devotional practice for mind discipline, because the mind is unstable and must be directed by a strong, steady divine principle.
- He states that by giving the reins of the mind to Krishna or to one’s chosen avatar/guru, one avoids chaos, confusion, and emotional being tossed about by the reactions of the world.

**18.13 Practical spiritual takeaway**
- The teacher’s core practical teaching is that the chart is not merely a matrix for prediction; it also tells us which divine principle and which inner discipline must govern the soul’s movement.
- The Sun and Lagna show the root of identity and the nature of self-expression; the Moon shows the mind and its fluctuations; the 10th house and Capricorn show the time-throne and the higher divine authority governing the life path.
- Therefore, real “strength” comes from inner regulation through mantra, worship, and commitment to the correct divine principle—rather than from a jewelry-based solution alone.

**17.4 Bilva-patra (Bilva leaves) remedy for close Sun-Venus conjunction**
- For a very tight (close-degree) Sun-Venus conjunction (flagged by a student as a specific concern) — recommended remedy: offer 108 Bilva (Bel) leaves to a Shivling, with a mantra recited over each leaf ("Om" is referenced as part of the process), performed every Monday. Duration: either the four Mondays of the Hindu month of Shravan (for a milder/normal-severity case), or every Monday continuously for one full year (for a more serious case).

**17.5 Vashikaran — restricted, legitimate-relationship use only**
- Vashikaran (a tantric influence/attraction practice) is explicitly restricted by the teacher to **legitimate, already-existing relationships only**: former spouse, current spouse, parent-child, or siblings.
- Explicitly stated as **not** to be used to create attraction/influence on someone with whom the native has no legitimate prior relationship.
- Recommended specifically for situations of frequent conflict/fighting between an already-married husband and wife, to help restore attraction and reduce conflict.
- (Specific simple Vashikaran techniques were promised for the next class session, not detailed in Part 2.)

**17.6 Prashna-specific note (flagged, not detailed in Part 2)**
- The teacher notes that horoscope-based marriage principles (from birth chart, marriage-time chart, and first-menstruation-timed chart for a female native) apply equivalently across all of these charts — but that Prashna (horary/question-time) charts carry some *additional*, Prashna-specific interpretive rules not applicable to natal analysis (one example given: Mars connected to the 7th house in a Prashna chart can indicate the timing of intimacy relative to the partner's menstrual cycle) — full Prashna-specific rules were deferred to a follow-up class.

## Notes for App Integration

- This document now covers **both Part 1 and Part 2 of Class 1** (full first-day lecture). Several topics remain explicitly flagged by the teacher as "to be covered in a later class" — most notably: full married-sannyasa treatment, complete family-member remedy permissions, detailed exceptions to the sign-matching rule, marriage/divorce **timing** (Dasha-based), refined Mangal Dosha rules, and full Prashna-specific rules. These are gaps to fill once further class transcripts are processed — do not let your app imply completeness on these topics yet.
- Several rules include explicit **exceptions and overrides** (Rule 1.4's house-lord supremacy principle; Rule 12.9's Rahu-in-7th exception; Rule 14.2's contradictory-indicator resolution; Rule 15.1's single-marriage overriding multi-marriage combinations) — when structuring this into a rules engine or RAG chunks, preserve the exception/override relationships between rules, not just the rules in isolation, or your app's answers may contradict the source material's own hierarchy of principles.
- Rules involving extreme/dramatic real-life examples (Rules 4.4, 4.6, 11.3, 12.4 anecdotes) were included by the teacher as illustrative edge cases, not as blanket predictions — consider flagging these as "illustrative" rather than "deterministic" in your app's tone when surfaced to end users, to avoid alarming users with a matching combination.
- **Methodological rule to preserve in your RAG/prompt layer** (Rule 14.1): planets, signs, and houses have distinct, non-interchangeable significations in this teacher's framework — a "Saturn Navamsha" reading must never be conflated with a "Capricorn/Aquarius sign" reading, even though the planet rules those signs. If your retrieval or LLM layer isn't instructed on this distinction, it risks generating answers that violate the source material's core interpretive methodology.
- Cancellation/exception logic is treated as equally important as the "positive" combinations throughout both parts (the teacher repeatedly stresses "if I forget to tell you a cancellation, remind me") — your app's system prompt should instruct the LLM to always check for and mention relevant cancelling factors alongside any combination it surfaces to a user, rather than stating raw combinations as deterministic outcomes.

# Marriage Astrology Knowledge Base — Class 1, Follow-up Transcript

## 1. Course Philosophy and Matchmaking Method

**1.1 Course structure and teaching order**
- The teacher begins by saying that the course will teach the North Indian system, then the South Indian system, then the Kerala system, and finally the teacher's own research conclusions and examples.
- The teacher frames the course as a long integrated approach to marriage matching that includes philosophy, history, and personal research across systems, rather than a single textbook rule set.
- The teacher states that the same classical matching systems are not enough on their own, because even a good Gun Milan score can fail in practice, and a bad Gun Milan can sometimes still end in a good marriage.

**1.2 Why matchmaking is limited**
- Variations in traditions show that a one-size-fits-all chart match is not enough when real life includes personal behavior, family background, economics, social structure, and timing.
- In practice, the teacher emphasizes that the chart should be treated as one input in a wider decision process, and that the marriage of two people involves more than astrological scoring.
- The teacher says that North Indian Gun Milan is only part of the diagnosis and does not by itself determine marriage outcomes.

**1.3 Free will, karma, and timing**
- The teacher explains that astrology provides a probabilistic reading of outcomes, not absolute certainty.
- The teacher distinguishes prediction from intervention: an event may be probable, but the human choice to marry earlier or later, to accept or reject a partner, or to pursue a remedy is a form of free will.
- The teacher states that the way one responds to an astrological pattern can influence how strongly the result affects a person’s life.

**1.4 Remedies and their categories**
- The teacher distinguishes three forms of remedy: psychological, spiritual, and astrological.
- Psychological remedy means changing one’s behavior and way of responding to the pattern so the effect is less damaging.
- Spiritual remedy is described as more powerful than psychological correction and can open new opportunities or help remove suffering through grace, faith, discipline, and guru guidance.
- Astrological remedies include muhurta, vastu, and matchmaking choices, with the understanding that the chart is not destiny but a map of tendencies.

**1.5 Muhurta and planning**
- Muhurta is not just a last-minute date search. It must be planned from the beginning of the project or relationship, especially for events such as births, interviews, marriages, surgeries, or major life transitions.
- The teacher repeatedly emphasizes that choosing a good muhurta near the final moment cannot correct a weak foundation.
- The teacher argues that the best timing for a life event is built from the beginning, and that tampering with the event at the end can create worse problems than the original chart indicates.

## 2. Matchmaking as a Relationship Diagnosis

**2.1 Matchmaking is not only a score**
- The teacher stresses that horoscopes should be matched only after obtaining necessary personal background information on the two people: family background, education, habits, health, wealth, temperament, values, and social behavior.
- Matchmaking should be a diagnosis of compatibility in behavior, temperament, and life flow, not a purely mechanical score of points.
- A very strong external attraction or emotional attachment cannot replace chart compatibility.

**2.2 Best person and best fit**
- The teacher explains that an individual may have many possible life partners from a social or emotional perspective, but the chart will define the available or acceptable opportunities.
- He reminds that there are limits: the decision is neither pure chance nor pure free will.
- A high-quality match is a combination of a good chart pattern plus mutual intention and correct behavior, rather than only the emotional chemistry of the couple.

## 3. Rashi, Nakshatra, and Name-Based Readings

**3.1 Ascendant and Moon both matter**
- The teacher says the ascendant is more important than the Moon for matching because the ascendant represents the body, the event-pattern, and the life activity.
- The Moon represents mind, inner temperament, and emotional synchronization.
- In practice, the teacher says the ascendant should be used as the primary reference point for marriage matching, with the Moon used as a secondary support or cross-check.

**3.2 Name-based resonance**
- The teacher introduces the idea that a person’s name can be mapped to a sign and nakshatra through the name’s first letter and sound.
- Name resonance should be checked as a supplementary matching factor, particularly when one chart is weak and the other strong.
- Name changes are described as an important remedial lever to align the person more favorably with the chart or life pattern.

## 4. Planetary Friendship and Matching Logic

**4.1 Planetary friendship is a core cancellation system**
- Planetary friendship between the lords of the ascendant or Moon chart of the two people can cancel or weaken many matching defects.
- According to the teacher’s teaching, the planets have a friendship and enmity map that should be applied consistently rather than mechanically.
- In the practical lecture, the teacher gives a friendship map including:
  - Sun friendly with Jupiter, and inimical to Moon, Mars, Mercury, Venus, Saturn.
  - Moon friendly with Jupiter and Venus, and inimical to Sun, Mars, Mercury, Saturn.
  - Mars friendly with Mercury and Venus, and inimical to Sun, Moon, Jupiter, Saturn.
  - Mercury friendly with Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn.
  - Jupiter friendly with Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Saturn.
  - Venus friendly with Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and inimical to Sun and Moon.
  - Saturn friendly with Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and inimical to Sun, Moon, Mars.
- This friendship table should not be applied mechanically without considering the stage of life, house position, and actual chart condition.

**4.2 Planetary friendship as a major rescue rule**
- If the ascendant lords or Moon lords of the two charts are friendly, many non-favorable sign or guna combinations can be softened or canceled.
- If they are dissimilar or hostile, marriage mismatches become more dangerous, because not only the family and economics but also attachment and mutual respect become harder to sustain.

## 5. Nakshatra, Tara, and Dosha Positioning

**5.1 Tara as a timing and compatibility factor**
- The teacher and the lecture transcript structure Tara calculations using the 27 nakshatras arranged into 9 groups, with certain groups considered more favorable or unfavorable.
- The bad Tara groupings are identified as those connected to danger, obstacle, and damage-like outcomes, especially the 3rd, 5th, 7th, 12th, 14th, 16th, 21st, 23rd, and 25th in one system of counting.
- In the transcript, the teacher notes that the 3rd, 5th, and 7th kinds of Tara especially should be especially handled with caution in life decisions and during marriage timing.

**5.2 Nadi and health/fertility caution**
- In the North Indian matching structure, Nadi dosa is treated as a serious issue affecting health, childbirth, and the couple’s physical life.
- The teacher emphasizes that where health or childbirth is harmed, the chart should be investigated using the involved houses rather than only the matching points.
- Nadi is not just text and score; it is a serious compatibility category that is expected to affect the life of the couple.

**5.3 Veda and marriage obstruction**
- Veda is described in the transcript as an obstruction between two nakshtatras or horoscope patterns that is not a simple “yes/no” point but a relation-based obstacle.
- The teacher mentions “Veda” as a structural obstruction that can disrupt marriage and needs to be checked with the same care as Nadi and Tara.

## 6. Vashya and Sign Control

**6.1 Vashya and household control**
- The teacher gives a practical chart of control for signs: sign categories such as human, animal, four-footed, watery, insect-like, and so on.
- The teacher frames Vashya as an issue of who will influence whom in a relationship, not just as a sign classification.
- The ideal relationship is where the partner with the stronger or more favorable house and chart configuration controls the partner with a weaker chart, rather than the weaker partner controlling the stronger one.

**6.2 Broader household application**
- The teacher says that Vashya principles extend from marriage to workplace and family relationships, especially where one person must give direction and another must follow.
- The same principle can be used to understand a social, bureaucratic, or family hierarchy in terms of which chart has stronger authority or capability.

## 7. Yoni and Financial-Prana Logic

**7.1 Yoni as a relationship and finance category**
- The lecture introduces Yoni as a category that influences not only sexuality but also family finances, household flow, and the practical management of wealth.
- It is described as a deeper compatibility mechanism than a simple data point because the nature of the animal/yoni categories tells how two people work together in household and money matters.
- Different yoni categories are compared based on compatibility of temperament, aggression, and financial style.

**7.2 Nature of pairing categories**
- The teacher explains that categories such as lion/tiger, animal, reptile, four-footed vegetarian, and bird-like groups each indicate a different relationship dynamic in domestic life.
- This is a practical rule of household flow: two people whose yoni categories support one another are more likely to understand the family economy and live in manageable financial rhythm.

## 8. Remedies and Spiritual Practice

**8.1 Spiritual remedy as relationship repair**
- The teacher explains that spiritual remedies can change the experienced form of an event, not merely the outer expression.
- Example: if a bad financial period is indicated, a spiritual practice may create broader support from family, community, teachers, or divine grace, reducing the raw burden of the event.

**8.2 Psychological correction and humility**
- The teacher says that many marriage problems are less about destiny and more about behavior: one must avoid aggression, alcohol, ego, contempt, neglect, and habit-bound conflict.
- A chart’s negative signal can be converted into a better lived experience through behavior changes, detachment, prayer, charity, gratitude, and humility.

**8.3 General remedial institutions**
- The teacher recommends charity, donation, service, temple work, and compassion toward the poor, the sick, or those in captivity.
- This matches his general idea that remedies should work not only for the self but for the social and family ecology around the marriage.

## 9. Practical Guidance for Marriage Selection

**9.1 Select on the grounds of both chart and character**
- The teacher says it is not enough to know the score. One must know the qualities of the person: character, wealth, family upbringing, health, habits, and social environment.
- The teacher gives an example that even a beloved match can fail if finances, family pressure, or practical living conditions are not compatible.

**9.2 Good charting does not mean one should ignore personal consent**
- The teacher emphasizes that the chart is a tool, not a prison: a good chart can still fail if the couple’s values and behavior do not align.
- A spiritually or psychologically wrong approach to the marriage will create stress even where the chart has a positive frame.

# Marriage Astrology Knowledge Base — Class 2, Part 1

## 1. South Indian System — Matchmaking Components

**1.1 South Indian components**
- South Indian matchmaking includes: Dinam, Ganam, Mahendram, Sthiradhrigam, Yoni, Rashi, Rashiadhipati, Rajju, Vedha.
- The same Tara logic used in North Indian matching is also used in the South Indian system.
- The South Indian system adds a specific exception to the standard Tara sequence: 12th, 14th, 16th Tara are not uniformly bad; only the first, fourth, and third quarters of those nakshatras are considered bad, while the second quarter is comparatively acceptable.
- Likewise, 21st, 23rd, and 25th Tara are treated more leniently in the South Indian exception, but the teacher cautions that it is safer to avoid these cases altogether unless a strong love compatibility signal exists.

**1.2 Mahendra**
- Mahendra is related to prosperity and children.
- Mahendra matching is tested by checking the Moon or Ascendant nakshatra of one partner against the nakshatra of the other at specific distances: 4th, 10th, 14th, 16th, 19th, 22nd, and 25th from the spouse's nakshatra.
- The teacher emphasizes that only the 4th, 10th, and 19th nakshatra relations are genuine prosperity-giving Mahendra positions; the others are not pure or are contaminated by Tara and Nadi-type contradictions.
- When Mahendra is properly present, it supports childbirth, fertility, and marital prosperity.

**1.3 Sri-Dhirgam / Sthira-Dhirgam**
- Sthira-Dhirgam is the “stability and consistency” factor and is compared to Nadi-like relationship flow.
- If the female/higher-priority chart is more than 12 nakshatras from the male chart, then the marriage becomes a problem of family dominion over the relationship, especially in traditional family structures.
- The teacher presents Sthira-Dhirgam as favoring mutual astrological support and respect, and as a key indicator of continuity rather than separation.

**1.4 Yoni and Gana**
- Gana and Yoni are treated as common principles between North and South systems.
- Yoni is gendered and can be used as a check on companionship, money use, and household balance.
- When the linga/yoni category leads to male–male or female–female pairing, the teacher flags it as an inherently difficult pattern for stable household flow.

**1.5 Rashi and Rashiadhipati**
- Rashi and Rashiadhipati matching are shared principles between North and South systems.
- The teacher restates that 6–8 and 5–9 sign relationships are especially important for interpreting friendship, variety, and compatibility, though the final use remains relational and contextual.

**1.6 Rajju and Veda**
- Rajju (snake-like nakshatra grouping) and Vedha are specific relationship obstructions in the South Indian framing.
- Rajju checks whether the couple is born in a matching group of nakshatras (snake-line pattern) and states that people born in the same Rajju group develop stress, disease, or misfortune together.
- Vedha indicates obstructive nakshatra connection and is treated as a serious relationship-affecting factor; if couple charts place planets or moon signs in Veda-making positions, the marriage is advised against.

## 2. Vashya / Vasya Systems

**2.1 South Indian Vashya**
- The South Indian Vashya scheme places one sign over another; the teacher gives a standard sign-control table: Aries controls Leo and Scorpio; Taurus controls Cancer and Libra; Gemini controls Virgo; Cancer controls Taurus and Scorpio; Leo controls Libra; Virgo controls Gemini and Pisces; Libra controls Capricorn and Virgo; Scorpio controls Cancer; Sagittarius controls Pisces; Capricorn controls Aries and Aquarius; Aquarius controls Aries; Pisces controls Capricorn.
- A strong controlling sign can restrain or neutralize a bad planet sign; a bad sign which is controlled by a strong benefic sign can reduce the malefic result.
- The teacher insists this is a practical balancing principle, useful for understanding whether a difficult planet or sign is actually able to drag the relationship down or is being held under control.

**2.2 Vashya as household prosperity principle**
- Vashya matching is not merely a personality test; it can reinforce prosperity, family comfort, and the ability of one person’s chart to support the other.
- The sign with the better horoscope should be allowed to control the sign of the weaker chart.

## 3. Tara and household misfortune

**3.1 Tara logic**
- Tara is the quality of auspiciousness; in practical use, the 9 Tara rows are Janma, Sampat, Vipata, Shatru, Pratyak, Vadh, and Maitra/Athi-Maitra variants.
- Bad/obstructing Tara positions are 3rd, 5th, 7th, 12th, 14th, 16th, 21st, 23rd, and 25th, depending on the teacher’s South-Indian exceptions and cautionary framework.

**3.2 Practical caution**
- Even if a couple has a strong relationship, a bad Tara match or Veda match should not be ignored. The teacher advises not to accept a match purely on the basis of emotional bonding, because the relationship may still become stressful or difficult.
- The relationship should be judged by chart compatibility, and the emotional bond must be verified with proven compatibility patterns (e.g., Khetguna/role-specific attraction patterns) rather than taken at face value.

## 4. Relationship and marriage suitability

**4.1 Love first, but verify**
- The teacher insists that the idea “if two people love each other, matchmaking is unnecessary” is false.
- Love alone may not be enough; the chart must still show compatibility, especially in the form of Khetguna, Vashya, Tara, Veda, and household sign relation.
- If a couple is truly emotionally compatible and has Khetguna or other love-supporting arrangements, the South Indian exception to certain Tara/Veda positions may be applied more leniently.

**4.2 Dasha Sandhi caution**
- Dasha Sandhi periods are dangerous because they are periods of transition where one dasha is ending and another beginning. They are not isolated to the native’s career, and can destabilize marriage and finances.
- When both partners are in Dasha Sandhi, the marriage becomes especially vulnerable, even if the chart pair otherwise seems positive.

## 5. Kerala and later systems

**5.1 Kerala system framing**
- The Kerala system prioritizes the female chart as the primary lens.
- The teacher describes Kerala-style matching as a later extension of the same practical principles, adding the idea of marriage as a household support system that must be interpreted through the relation of the female/native’s chart to house and planet lords.
- A female/native’s chart is often read through 7th-house related conditions, marriage harmony, and family support, while the male chart may be interpreted from a more supportive/prosperity-facing lens.

**5.2 Graha Sutra and marriage vows**
- The teacher frames marriage not only as chart matching but also as a vow and way of life. The match should support family responsibility, mutual respect, and household order.
- This is connected to the Graha Sutram and marriage rites where duties and social behavior are interpreted as chart-supporting actions.

## 6. Additional chart-level commentary from the transcript

**6.1 Paapa Samyam**
- Papasamyam is a balancing of the malefic pattern in both charts. It is not a substitute for 7th-house or Khetguna checks, but an additional balancing principle: if the total malefic burden in both charts is approximately equal, the marriage is more stable.
- Differences greater than a threshold of roughly 25% are treated as incompatible by the teacher’s practical rubric.

**6.2 Khetguna**
- Khetguna is presented as the most important long-term relationship guide for true compatibility and spouse-fortune.
- The spouse’s Ascendant or Moon sign should fall into the sign of exaltation, debilitation, occupation, or trine of the native’s 7th lord.
- If this does not happen, the couple may struggle to form or sustain a lasting marriage even if the relationship is long-standing.

**6.3 Dasa Sandhi practical alert**
- Dasa Sandhi is a transition period and must be checked against the planet sequence in the native’s dhasha, especially when two charts show similar timing pressure or relationship timing pressure.
- These timings create stress that can be mistaken for a chart mismatch, but are really transition-period stress patterns.

If one person has burden in the 7th and another in the 8th, it is not automatically balance but depends on the timing and the planet involved.”

**7.1 Weak dasha and Dasha Sandhi vulnerability**
- The teacher defines a weak dasha as a period when the native is effectively vulnerable and exposed, especially in marriage, finances, and emotional stability.
- Dasha Sandhi is treated as a dangerous transition period where one dasha ends and another starts; this is not a neutral time and should be checked as a marriage-risk factor.
- If both partners are in vulnerable, enemy, or Maraka Dashas at the same time, the marriage is treated as under severe stress even when the charts appear otherwise acceptable.

**7.2 Mangala Dosha and 7th/8th-house logic**
- In the follow-up lecture, malefic influence in the 7th, 8th, 4th, 12th, 2nd, 1st, and 6th houses is treated as relevant to marital vulnerability, not only Mars by itself.
- The teacher explicitly warns that the dasha of the malefic matters as much as the chart; a chart can be balanced on paper but still break during the dasha of a malefic.
- The 8th house is especially emphasized as the house of spouse loss or serious marriage trouble, while the 7th house is the house of marriage and widowhood in a female chart.

**7.3 House-lord cancellation and relationship balance**
- The teacher insists on the principle that aspect equals conjunction and that a planet in the same sign in D9 is treated as equivalent to a planet in the same house in D1 for practical truth-testing.
- Cancellation or neutralization is only valid if the balance is real and not a false equalization; if the counts are equal or the determining lords are in conflict, the cancellation does not hold.
- The teacher’s practical filter is to check the strength of the Lagna and Moon, then judge which house is being afflicted and which lord is actually activating the result.

**7.4 Child-bearing and lack-of-children logic**
- The teacher gives a practical rule: if the 5th lord is weak, afflicted by the 6th/8th/12th lords, or the 5th house is not supported by benefics, fertility or child-bearing can be compromised.
- Conversely, strong benefic support, strong Jupiter, and favorable Mars placement increase the chance of children and the continuity of the lineage.
- The teacher warns that a mere “good-looking” chart is not enough; the dasha timing and the actual 5th-house support must both be checked to assess fertility and child survival.

**7.5 Marriage timing and decision-making**
- The practical conclusion of the transcript is that astrological timing (Dasha, Dasha Sandhi, Maraka planets, and Lagna-Lord enemy Dashas) must be checked before finalizing a match.
- The teacher repeatedly argues that chart compatibility without timing analysis can lead to false reassurance; emotional attraction and social compatibility are not substitutes for chart-timing integrity.
- The matching process therefore includes: basic chart behavior, house-lord balance, pancha-bhava support, and the timing of the active dasha interval in both charts.

This material is very detailed and cannot be covered in one hurried pass. That is why I am expanding this series into further levels.”

**8.1 Sages’ matches and their practical use**
- The teacher says the 18 sages’ matches are not to be discarded just because they are less popular in modern matchmaking; they remain part of the deeper traditional model.
- The categories listed in the transcript—Mahendra, Gana, Dina, Yoni, Srija, Bhootlinga, Varga, Jatikut, Pakshikut, Yogini, Gothra, and related measures—reflect a more subtle compatibility model than a simple numeric score.
- The teacher stresses that many of the later matching systems simplified or dropped these categories, but the older system remains informative when used carefully.

**8.2 Bhuta-Linga / Prana-Linga**
- Bhuta-Linga is described as a sexual-energy compatibility factor based on male-female constitutional rhythm, not only on sign type.
- The teacher frames sexual compatibility as the flow of prana and the health of the relationship; incorrect timing or incompatible energy flow can create bodily or psychic imbalance.
- If the male is born in a male nakshatra and the female in a female nakshatra, the pairing is considered favorable; if the energies are reversed or mismatched, the underlying energy flow is considered unstable.

**8.3 Gotra, Jati, and caste logic**
- The teacher includes gotra and jati as serious compatibility checks, particularly where family continuity and household purity are considered important.
- He stresses that caste-type compatibility is no longer a simple social rule in modern life, but in the older tradition it is part of the marriage diagnosis, especially when family structure and value alignment are important.
- The guide warns that these factors should not be treated as a social prejudice or a final verdict on a relationship; they are ancient compatibility principles, not an excuse to ignore character and personal suitability.

**8.4 Varga and divisional-chart match**
- The teacher states that varga matching is a deeper compatibility test than simple sign matching and should be checked using divisional chart compatibility and the ascendant or Moon sign relation across charts.
- Poor varga compatibility may create a marriage that survives by duty rather than affection, while good varga compatibility supports emotional and spiritual resonance.
- The teacher also discusses the use of name-based modifications as a practical remedial tool, but not as a replacement for real chart analysis.

**8.5 Rashi-lord friendship and Vashya control**
- The teacher reasserts that the old friendship tables remain important and should not be simplified away.
- In this framework, if the lords of the signs are friendly, many small mismatches can be softened; if they are hostile, the quality of the relationship is more likely to become strained.
- Vashya is treated as a practical relationship-control principle. The teacher corrects the older table and points out that the control map must be used with care rather than mechanically.

**8.6 Yogini Kuta and deeper compatibility**
- Yogini Kuta is described as a subtle compatibility factor that can indicate harmony, danger, or spiritual-psychic stress in the marriage.
- The teacher’s practical point is that even when a relationship appears socially suitable, a poor yogini pairing can bring instability or repeated trouble.
- This is one of the examples of the deeper traditional methods that later simplification tends to underweight or ignore.

**8.7 Timing, remedies, and the hierarchy of factors**
- The teacher repeatedly returns to the idea that no single matching factor should be viewed in isolation.
- The order of judgment is not purely mechanical: first chart fundamentals, then house-lord balance, then compatibility factors like Vashya, Veda, Tara, and Yogini, then Dasha timing, then practical remedies and life discipline.
- The aim is not to eliminate risk by a formula but to understand whether the couple has a stable life rhythm and whether timing is favorable.

>
> Speaker 1 (1:13:26): “The same-nakshatra match is not always a clean yes or no. Some same nakshatra combinations are good, some medium, and some destructive depending on pada and sign structure. So the deeper rule is not just ‘same nakshatra = bad’ or ‘same nakshatra = good.’ It depends on placement and relation.”
>
> Speaker 1 (1:19:47): “Yoni is the sexual and animal-symbolic matching system. The matching should be compatible, not merely same or opposite. Some yonis are friendly, some are inimical, some are neutral. This is a key part of the deeper astrology of physical compatibility.”
>
> Speaker 1 (1:31:22): “Graha Maitri and Bhakut are complementary checks. If one is missing, the other can sometimes rescue the match. But if both are poor, the relationship is exposed to recurring friction, emotional distance, and mental burn-out.”
>
> Speaker 1 (1:37:31): “Nadi and Veda are serious. Some forms of Nadi or Veda are not to be taken lightly. If a chart has a severe Veda or Nadi pattern and no substantial cancellation, the marriage should be treated as high-risk.”
>
> Speaker 1 (1:43:50): “The strongest chart-level checks are Veda, Raju, Bhakut, Graha Maitri, and timing. These should be used first to eliminate unsuitable matches. Only after that should you look at the more layered compatibility factors.”
>
> Speaker 1 (2:01:29): “The final principle is that if a match is very difficult but a strong positive omen or a highly supportive chart timing is present, it can still be accepted. But this requires both chart logic and practical discretion. Astrology is a guide, not a tyrant.”

**9.1 Free will and the purpose of matchmaking**
- The teacher frames matchmaking as a major arena for exercising free will. It is not pure destiny; it is a moment where people choose a compatible pattern, a stable family layout, and a better life path.
- The teacher repeatedly emphasizes that marriage is not just about attraction but also about how two people can sustain a life together under stress, timing, and temperament.
- Practical remedies are therefore not “magic”; they are applied intelligence plus disciplined behavior, especially when the chart is difficult.

**9.2 Guna and chart interpretation from the proper reference point**
- Guna matching should not be treated as a purely numerical table exercise. The teacher insists that body, mind, and energy must be read from the correct reference point.
- For emotional and mental compatibility, the Moon is more relevant; for physical compatibility and sexual durability, the ascendant and related houses are more relevant.
- If horoscopes are unavailable, name-based compatibility can be used as a temporary substitute, but the method should be consistent and not mixed with chart-based logic.

**9.3 Varnam, professions, and social temperament**
- Varnam is presented as a deep social temperament pattern, not just a caste label. Its purpose is to see the life orientation and household rhythm of the pair.
- The teacher says that the social category of a person can be approximated by profession, sign pattern, and dominant planetary placement when a caste label is not sufficient or is not socially applicable in the modern setting.
- The practical lesson is: same varna is safest, but a mismatched varna may still be workable if the life orientation and mutual temperament are appropriately balanced.

**9.4 Bhakut, Graha Maitri, and the hidden compatibility factors**
- The teacher gives repeated importance to Bhakut and Graha Maitri as the primary relationship checks after the gross matching factors are cleared.
- A mismatch in one of these can often be compensated by the other, but if both are poor, the couple is likely to experience chronic emotional strain.
- The teacher also introduces the “hidden” or “deeper” compatibility factors such as drishti, deha, rekha, and vidya-vinaya, which are not captured by standard tables but are highly relevant to a real marriage.

**9.5 Nadi, Veda, and serious compatibility warning signs**
- Nadi and Veda are treated as serious, not optional, compatibility issues. They are not merely decorative points on a chart.
- Where a severe Nadi or Veda is present without sufficient cancellation, the teacher treats the match as high-risk and often advises against marriage.
- The teacher also notes that several “same nakshatra” or “same sign” combinations look bad on first glance but can be acceptable when the pada, sign relation, and chart-level support are favorable.

**9.6 Timing, omens, and practical judgment**
- The teacher places a great emphasis on omens, the timing of consultation, and practical social judgment before final approval.
- A chart can be technically acceptable but still fail if the omens or life timing are poor or if the couple’s behavior is incompatible.
- The broader lesson is that astrology is a judgmental discipline, not a simplistic points system. Free will, behavior, and timing all matter.
